summarizeTools(pi: ExtensionAPI) {\n  return pi.getAllTools().map(tool => ({\n    name: tool.name,\n    source: tool.sourceInfo.source,\n    description: tool.description ?? \"\",\n    parameters: tool.parameters,\n  }));\n}\n\nfunction toDecision(reply: RouterReply): RouteDecision | null {\n  if (reply.ok !== true || !Array.isArray(reply.names)) return null;\n  const names = reply.names.filter((name): name is string => typeof name === \"string\");\n  const confidence = Number(reply.confidence);\n  if (!Number.isFinite(confidence)) return null;\n  return { names, confidence };\n}\n\nasync function routePrompt(prompt: string, tools: ReturnType<typeof summarizeTools>): Promise<RouteDecision | null> {\n  const external = tools.filter(tool => tool.source === \"mcp\");\n  if (external.length === 0) return null;\n\n  const controller = new AbortController();\n  const timer = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), ROUTER_TIMEOUT_MS);\n  try {\n    const controller.signal,\n    });\n    if (!response.ok) return null;\n    return toDecision(await response.json() as RouterReply);\n  } catch {\n    return null;\n  } finally {\n    clearTimeout(timer);\n  }\n}\n\nexport default function cactusReflex(pi: ExtensionAPI) {\n  let lastRoute: { selected: string[]; total: number; confidence?: number; mode: \"filtered\" | \"fail-open\" } | null = null;\n\n  pi.on(\"before_agent_start\", async event => {\n    const tools = summarizeTools(pi);\n    const allNames = tools.map(tool => tool.name);\n    const summaries: ToolSummary[] = tools.map(tool => ({\n      name: tool.name,\n      source: tool.source,\n      description: tool.description,\n    }));\n\n    const decision = await routePrompt(event.prompt, tools);\n    const selected = selectActiveToolNames(summaries, decision, event.prompt);\n    await pi.setActiveTools(selected);\n\n    lastRoute = {\n      selected,\n      total: allNames.length,\n      confidence: decision?.confidence,\n      mode: selected.length < allNames.length ? \"filtered\" : \"fail-open\",\n    };\n\n    pi.logger.debug(\"cactus-reflex route\", {\n      mode: lastRoute.mode,\n      confidence: lastRoute.confidence,\n      selected: selected.length,\n      total: allNames.length,\n    });\n  });\n\n  pi.on(\"session_shutdown\", async () => {\n    const names = pi.getAllTools().map(tool => tool.name);\n    await pi.setActiveTools(names);\n  });\n\n  pi.registerCommand(\"cactus-reflex\", {\n    description: \"Show Cactus reflex-router status for this OMP session\",\n    handler: async (_args, ctx) => {\n      const status = lastRoute\n        ? `Cactus reflex: ${lastRoute.mode}; ${lastRoute.selected.length}/${lastRoute.total} tools active${lastRoute.confidence === undefined ? \"\" : `; confidence ${lastRoute.confidence.toFixed(4)}`}`\n        : \"Cactus reflex: loaded; no prompt routed yet\";\n      ctx.ui.notify(status, \"info\");\n    },\n  });\n}\n","mode":"rewrite"}}

[835] assistant (text, 4521 chars)
One concrete measurement from the live OMP configuration: a GitHub-only request reduces the available tool descriptions from **23,639 to 11,118 characters**, a 53% reduction. That is not yet a measured reduction in tokens, latency, or total compute cost.

The larger issue remains Cactus itself: cold inference is too slow. I'm testing whether keeping its model loaded can eliminate that overhead without risking OMP stability.

The native benchmark has identified the bottleneck: with Needle kept in one process, five routing requests completed in **27–34 milliseconds each**, using approximately 121 MB of peak process memory.

That changes the repair: keep Needle resident in a crash-isolated worker instead of starting Python and loading its dependencies on every tool decision.

The resident-worker stress test has now completed **20 of 20 limited routing checks**, with a warm median of **26.7 ms** and no process crashes. That's strong evidence the repeated process startup—not Needle's actual routing speed—was causing the earlier delay.

I'm moving from the standalone benchmark to the supervised service integration. Until that's connected to OMP, those benchmark results do not count as deployment completion.

The replacement service has now passed its first live HTTP routing request: **97 ms**, with Needle selecting GitHub correctly. The worker was already running, and OMP itself remained separate from the native process.

I'm finishing the production connection and its failure-handling checks now.

The production launch service has now restarted with the resident-worker implementation. Its metrics confirm the new worker is alive. The earlier cold-start-per-request design is no longer what the production service is running.

The OMP extension has also been rebuilt to consult Needle on eligible ambiguous requests, with conservative fallbacks when confidence is insufficient.

The production stress test exposed another issue: although the resident worker is fast in isolation, live requests on the Mac took roughly **0.5–1.6 seconds**, and two oversized cases timed out.

I'm checking system contention and the worker's recovery behavior rather than accepting the isolated benchmark as representative. The extension's fail-open behavior is important here: a delayed or failed model decision must not remove necessary tools.

That scheduling test found the performance problem.

Changing the launch service from **Background** to **Standard** reduced the live Needle requests to **25–30 ms after warmup**, with the first request at 153 ms. The oversized request now fails open in 2 ms rather than tying up the worker.

This is measured through the production HTTP service, not just a standalone Python benchmark.

Crash recovery is now verified too. I deliberately terminated the native Needle worker, then ran the production routing checks again.

The supervising service stayed up, started a replacement worker, and successfully handled the subsequent requests. Its metrics show **two worker starts, 11 successful inference requests, zero native-response failures, and one live replacement worker**. The oversized requests were rejected safely before inference.

The extension has also passed the three important live OMP paths: ordinary startup loads it automatically; a single-tool request filters **25 tools to 9 in about 5 ms**; and a compound GitHub-plus-web request preserves all 25 tools.

An ambiguous request actually reached Needle, but its confidence was low, so OMP correctly retained the full tool set instead of guessing. I'm now checking provider-reported usage rather than equating shorter tool descriptions with actual savings.

We now have a provider-reported savings measurement from matched OMP prompts:

| Metric | Full tools | Filtered tools |
|---|---:|---:|
| Active tools | 25 | 9 |
| Prompt tokens, including cached reads | 15,078 | 12,686 |
| Reported request cost | $0.002287 | $0.001428 |

That's **2,392 fewer prompt tokens, or approximately 15.9%**, in this comparison.

The reported cost was also lower, but the two runs had different cache usage, so I will not attribute that entire cost difference to routing. 

The normal, unmodified OMP startup also exposed a separate blocker: its Laya Judge MCP plugin was configured to launch a Windows `.cmd` file on macOS.

I corrected the launcher to use the installed Python environment and bridge. A subsequent normal OMP session completed successfully, and its log shows the Laya Judge MCP commands being registered.

- Real OMP sessions successfully searched GitHub, searched the web, and returned actual results.

## 3. What stopped us from calling it production-ready?

This is the important discovery from the final tests.

Needle initially produced mostly low-confidence answers when given OMP's long tool descriptions. I experimented with shorter, purpose-built descriptions.

That raised confidence substantially, but **some high-confidence answers were wrong**.

For example, when asked to generate a GitHub image icon, Needle selected the GitHub tool instead of image generation, with approximately 85% reported confidence.

That means confidence alone is not sufficient to decide whether a tool can safely be hidden.

**Those shorter descriptions were experimental and have not been deployed into the active OMP extension.** The production extension still uses its original descriptions and conservative fallback behavior.

There's also the savings question. The safer configuration preserves 18 of 28 tools on a straightforward GitHub request. One matched comparison showed only about 0.8% fewer input tokens. That's a measured improvement, but not enough to demonstrate meaningful overall computational savings.

## 4. Why did parts of the previous turn still say "running"?

Those messages came from individual remote-terminal commands. Some were launched as longer-running processes, and their displayed status wasn't always updated after they finished.

They do **not** mean I have an autonomous engineering session continuing to work after I finish answering.

The two processes intentionally still running are the router service and its resident Needle worker. They're supposed to stay online.

I should have separated those facts from the temporary test-process messages.

## 5. Where we actually stand

My next engineering move is to address that selection problem directly: keep questionable Needle decisions advisory-only, validate routing against representative OMP workloads, then retest accuracy, latency, and computational savings before signing off.

And one distinction matters: **this work is specifically the Oh My Pi/Cactus micro-routing integration.** It does not establish production readiness for Dottie, GrizzOS, OpenRig, or God's Eye View.

That's the actual state of the work, without the running commentary or the premature victory laps.

[1277] assistant (text, 378 chars)
The first routing benchmark is complete. Gemma chose the intended tool on all eight simple test cases, but the CPU-only responses took roughly 1.6–3.7 seconds. That's too slow to invoke indiscriminately.

I'm staging a guarded router now: straightforward requests stay on the fast path, Gemma handles eligible ambiguous choices, and uncertain or slow results preserve all tools.

## Durable run state
`bootstrap/initialize_run_state.py` writes the active run directory path to `.current_run`. Each run contains:

`MISSION.json`, `STATE.json`, `CHECKPOINT.md`, `EVENTS.jsonl`, `COMMANDS.jsonl`, `EVIDENCE/`, `ACCEPTANCE.json`, `RECOVERY.json`.

Before each consequential mutation, record intended change, observed pre-state, authority, expected result, rollback, and evidence path. After restart: **READ DURABLE STATE → INSPECT REALITY → COMPARE → RECONCILE → ONLY THEN CONTINUE.**

## Authority
Default unattended authority is: OBSERVE, RETRIEVE, COMPARE, REASON, INVESTIGATE, COLLECT EVIDENCE, REPORT, ALERT. Mutations require pre-existing authority and must be bounded, reversible, and idempotent. No component may grant itself authority, weaken TLS, broaden Proxmox privilege, invent credentials, expose arbitrary public ports, delete evidence, or rewrite identity/memory policy.

## Proxmox
Production Proxmox is in scope unless live discovery proves the canonical production substrate already exists elsewhere and satisfies the same requirements. Use:

- **operator lane:** ProxmoxMCP-Plus for authorized lifecycle/storage/snapshot/backup/rollback/jobs;
- **observer lane:** sergelogvinov/proxmox-mcp for independent inventory and post-change verification.

The builder does not certify itself. IDs, addresses, bridges, storage pools and capacities are resolved from live inventory—never from this packet.

## Completion
Packet execution is complete only when the running production system has evidence for service supervision/reboot survival, persistent canonical state, persistent Dottie and AEGIS identities, real inference and dispatch, real evidence provenance, authority enforcement, private-memory isolation, GEV projection, restart reconciliation, backup/restore, degraded-provider behavior, independent infrastructure verification, and a field mission set that continues when interactive OMP/OpenRig is closed.

A real blocker is recorded as `BLOCKED_REQUIRES_OPERATOR` with the smallest concrete operator action. A blocked branch does not stop safe independent work elsewhere.

#!/bin/sh
set -u
ROOT=$(CDPATH= cd -- "$(dirname -- "$0")/.." && pwd)
RUN=$(cat "$ROOT/.current_run" 2>/dev/null || true)
if [ -z "$RUN" ] || [ ! -d "$RUN" ]; then echo "run state missing; run initialize_run_state.py" >&2; exit 2; fi
OUT="$RUN/EVIDENCE/preflight.txt"
: > "$OUT"
log(){ printf '%s\n' "$*" | tee -a "$OUT"; }
log "GMRI production preflight"
log "utc=$(date -u '+%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ')"
log "host=$(hostname 2>/dev/null || true)"
log "pwd=$PWD"
for c in python3 git gh node npm omp openrig ollama ssh rsync jq curl docker podman xcodebuild swift; do
  if command -v "$c" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    log "FOUND $c=$(command -v "$c")"
    case "$c" in
      omp|openrig|ollama|python3|git|gh|node|npm|docker|podman|swift) "$c" --version >> "$OUT" 2>&1 || true ;;
      xcodebuild) xcodebuild -version >> "$OUT" 2>&1 || true ;;
    esac
  else
    log "MISSING $c"
  fi
done
if command -v omp >/dev/null 2>&1; then { echo '--- omp --help ---'; omp --help; } >> "$OUT" 2>&1 || true; fi
if command -v openrig >/dev/null 2>&1; then { echo '--- openrig --help ---'; openrig --help; } >> "$OUT" 2>&1 || true; fi
if command -v ollama >/dev/null 2>&1; then { echo '--- ollama list ---'; ollama list; } >> "$OUT" 2>&1 || true; fi
printf '%s\n' "$OUT"
#!/usr/bin/env python3
import argparse, json, os, secrets
from pathlib import Path
from datetime import datetime, timezone
p=argparse.ArgumentParser(); p.add_argument('--runs-root',required=True); a=p.parse_args()
root=Path(a.runs_root).resolve(); root.mkdir(parents=True,exist_ok=True)
now=datetime.now(timezone.utc); rid='prod-'+now.strftime('%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ')+'-'+secrets.token_hex(4)
r=root/rid; (r/'EVIDENCE').mkdir(parents=True)
def dump(n,o): (r/n).write_text(json.dumps(o,indent=2,sort_keys=True)+'\n')
dump('MISSION.json',{'run_id':rid,'mission':'GMRI production deployment','created_at':now.isoformat(),'hard_gate':'2026-10-08T23:59:00 local','status':'DISCOVERY'})
dump('STATE.json',{'run_id':rid,'state':'DISCOVERY','last_reconciled_at':None,'component_locks':{},'in_flight':[]})
(r/'CHECKPOINT.md').write_text('# Checkpoint\n\nDiscovery initialized. No production mutation recorded yet.\n')
(r/'EVENTS.jsonl').write_text(json.dumps({'event':'RUN_CREATED','at':now.isoformat(),'run_id':rid})+'\n')
(r/'COMMANDS.jsonl').write_text('')
dump('ACCEPTANCE.json',{'overall':'NOT_RUN','gates':{}})
dump('RECOVERY.json',{'sequence':['READ_DURABLE_STATE','INSPECT_REALITY','COMPARE','RECONCILE','CONTINUE'],'rollback_refs':[]})
packet=Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]; (packet/'.current_run').write_text(str(r)+'\n')
print(r)

# GMRI Production Deployment Mission

## Objective
By Thursday 08 October 2026 at 23:59 local time, produce the strongest evidence-backed operational deployment of **GrizOS + Dottie Hill + AEGIS / Howling Commandos** on GMRI-authorized infrastructure. Friday through Saturday, the deployed system must perform useful bounded watch work while the operator is away.

## Production-first rule
Production services are the object under test. Tests, canaries, fixtures and smoke checks may verify the product; none of them may substitute for a required production service, live data source, persistent intelligence, canonical state store, real model inference, or deployed endpoint.

## Existing-code-first rule
Inspect and integrate the strongest real GMRI implementation before writing new product code. New code is permitted for missing production adapters, configuration, migrations, deployment mechanics, or requirements not present in the real implementation. Every new code path must identify the missing requirement it closes.

## Required product boundaries
- **GrizOS**: operating environment/control plane for canonical world state, intelligence registration, identity boundaries, authority, provenance, durable mission/run state, capability routing, endpoint registration, receipts, continuity/reconciliation and GEV projection boundaries.
- **Dottie Hill**: persistent digital intelligence whose identity survives replacement of model/provider/worker/framework/tool/renderer/process/host; watch officer, provenance librarian, semantic translator, adversarial reader, institutional memory within explicit boundaries; not final authority.
- **AEGIS / Howling Commandos**: separate persistent intelligences with distinct identities, private memory, salience, competence, trust/failure histories, authority and operational histories. They are not prompts, personas, disposable workers, or one model identity wearing five names.
- **GEV**: first-class macro spatial plane and projection of canonical GrizOS world state; never canonical identity/memory/truth/mission authority.
- **OMP/OpenRig**: engineering shipyard only; not canonical state and not a field-runtime dependency.

## Identity invariants
Persist for Dottie at minimum: `intelligence_id`, `identity_manifest_ref`, `lineage_version`, `memory_namespace_ref`, `authority_profile_ref`, `relationship_state_ref`, `active_model_binding`, `model_binding_history`.

Preserve `intelligence_id = persistent intelligence` and `worker_id = disposable execution environment`. Destroying or replacing a worker must not destroy an intelligence.

## AEGIS differentiation
- Dugan: people / action / consequences. Humor and seriousness are coupled; humor is a load-bearing regulatory behavior, not evidence that stakes are low.
- Jones: context / legitimacy / competing obligations.
- Morita: actual problem vs bad assumption.
- Falsworth: obligation / institution / continuity / succession.
- Dernier: local action when formal machinery fails.

No GMRI subsystem named `Octa` may be created.

## Required field acceptance
Acceptance must demonstrate the deployed system with real services, real inference, real approved sources, actual durable state, actual authority enforcement, actual restart/reconciliation, actual memory isolation, actual GEV projection, actual backup/restore and independent Proxmox observation. Detailed gates are in `ACCEPTANCE.md`.
# Live Discovery

The packet does not hard-code current IP addresses, VM/LXC IDs, bridges, storage pools, credentials, installed OMP/OpenRig syntax, model availability, Xcode schemes or live service state.

At packet-build time the authorized Desktop Commander endpoint `gmri-mac.local` was observed **offline**, so the packet builder could not establish current local worktrees or installed OMP/OpenRig/Ollama versions. That state is an observation, not permission to guess. First execution re-runs discovery and records the live result.

`REPO_OBSERVATIONS.json` contains GitHub observations captured during this build. They are navigation evidence, not a declaration that any repository is the canonical GrizOS implementation. Local worktrees and newer commits win when observed and reconciled with the product sources.
# Architecture Constitution

## GrizOS
GrizOS is the operating environment and control plane. It owns/governs shared canonical world state, intelligence registration, identity boundaries, authority, provenance, durable mission state, capability routing, endpoint registration, native receipts, continuity/reconciliation and projection boundaries. It is not Dottie, a model, a renderer, a chatbot wrapper, OpenRig or GEV.

Governing invariants: technology adapts to the human; intent before interface; world state outranks screen state; uncertainty remains uncertainty; receipts are native; local capability remains meaningful during WAN/cloud loss; external data is not truth merely because it was ingested; implementation is replaceable where behavior is preserved.

## Dottie Hill
Dottie is a persistent intelligence and watch officer. Model/provider/worker/tool/framework/process/host changes are compute substitution, not identity replacement. Her communication/behavioral lineage uses Maria Hill as a source architecture without theatrical role-play. Preserve anti-sycophancy, semantic compression, evidentiary dissent, mandatory disconfirmation, graduated authority, BLUF/minimum sufficient framing and the operator's vocabulary.

## AEGIS
AEGIS members are separate persistent intelligences. Differences must emerge from source-grounded salience/judgment/trust/failure architectures, not assigned job titles plus stylistic accents. Findings and dissent remain attributable.

## State separation
Keep `IDENTITY`, `MEMORY`, `EVIDENCE`, `FINDING`, and `WORLD STATE` distinct. Enforce `retrieved != remembered`, `observed != believed`, `believed != institutionally accepted`, and `displayed in GEV != canonically true`.

## GEV / spatial
GEV is a macro-scale projection plane. Bridge Crossing preserves canonical object IDs, relationships, provenance and mission state while moving between global and local spatial contexts. A rendered object is never promoted to canonical truth merely because it is visible.

[L2]   "content": "# Reproducible Builds — JARVIS Native Runtime\n\n**Operator:** Robert \"Grizzly\" Hanson <me@grizzlymedicine.org>  \n**Applies to:** `apple_native/` — C++ runtime, Mac Cockpit, iOS/watchOS companion apps\n\n---\n\n## TL;DR\n\n| Build phase | Reproducible? | Blocker |\n|-------------|:-------------:|---------|\n| C++ runtime compile (CMake / Xcode) | ✅ Yes (with pins) | Timestamp in object files — strip with `-Wl,-no_uuid` or accept |\n| Swift compilation | ✅ Yes (with pins) | Swift embeds build-time UUID per module; deterministic with same toolchain |\n| Asset catalog processing | ⚠️ Mostly | `actool` embeds a timestamp in compiled `.car` files |\n| Code signing (development) | ❌ No | Timestamp in signature; entitlement list snapshot at sign time |\n| Code signing (release/notarization) | ❌ No | Apple notarization staple embeds Apple's timestamp |\n| Provisioning profile selection | ❌ No | Profile selected at sign time by Xcode; UUID changes with each re-provision |\n| Notarization staple | ❌ No | Apple-generated; non-reproducible by design |\n\n**Practical stance:** Pin the toolchain. Capture build metadata. Accept that the signed artifact\nis not byte-for-byte reproducible. The *source-to-object* compilation step IS reproducible given\nthe same toolchain, SDK, and flags.\n\n---\n\n## Required Environment\n\nPin these versions for any build that enters the evidentiary record:\n\n| Component | Pinned value | How to verify |\n|-----------|-------------|---------------|\n| Xcode | See `BUILD_ENV.xcconfig` | `xcodebuild -version` |\n| macOS SDK | See `BUILD_ENV.xcconfig` | `xcrun --sdk macosx --show-sdk-version` |\n| Swift toolchain | See `BUILD_ENV.xcconfig` | `swift --version` |\n| macOS (build host) | See `BUILD_ENV.xcconfig` | `sw_vers -productVersion` |\n| libc++ | Pinned by SDK version | Ships with Xcode CLT |\n| CommonCrypto | Pinned by SDK version | Ships with macOS SDK |\n\nCurrent build environment (captured at doc generation):\n\n```\n# Current machine — update BUILD_ENV.xcconfig to match your release environment\nJARVIS_REQUIRED_XCODE_VERSION   = (run: xcodebuild -version)\nJARVIS_REQUIRED_SDK_VERSION     = (run: xcrun --sdk macosx --show-sdk-version)\nJARVIS_REQUIRED_SWIFT_VERSION   = (run: swift --version | head -1)\nJARVIS_REQUIRED_MACOS_VERSION   = (run: sw_vers -productVersion)\n```\n\nCreate `apple_native/BUILD_ENV.xcconfig` with pinned values and import it into\nthe Xcode project as a base configuration. This file should be committed and is\nthe authoritative record of the build environment for a given release.\n\n---\n\n## Where Builds CAN Be Reproducible\n\n### C++ Runtime (JARVISNativeRuntime)\n\nThe C++ source (`JARVISNativeRuntime.cpp`) depends only on:\n- C++ standard library (ships with SDK)\n- CommonCrypto (ships with SDK)\n\nNo vendored dependencies. No generated code. No macros with `__DATE__` / `__TIME__` unless\nexplicitly added.\n\n**Determinism requirements:**\n```\n# Flags that help determinism (add to CXXFLAGS):\n-ffile-prefix-map=$(SRCROOT)=.      # strip absolute paths from debug info\n-Wno-builtin-macro-redefined\n# Avoid if possible (not strictly needed):\n# __DATE__, __TIME__, __TIMESTAMP__ macros — exclude from source\n```\n\n**UUID stripping** (accepts non-byte-identical binaries but removes timestamp):\n```\n# In CMakeLists.txt or Xcode build settings:\nOTHER_LDFLAGS = -Wl,-no_uuid\n```\n\n### Swift Package (JARVISCompanion)\n\nSwift compilation is deterministic given:\n1. Same Swift toolchain version\n2. Same SDK version  \n3. Same source files (no generated output that embeds timestamps)\n4. `-wmo` (whole-module-optimization) mode for release builds\n\n```bash\n# Deterministic Swift build invocation:\nswift build \\\n    --configuration release \\\n    --triple arm64-apple-macosx14.0 \\\n    -Xswiftc -wmo \\\n    -Xswiftc -Onone   # or -O for release\n```\n\n---\n\n## Where Builds CANNOT Be Reproducible\n\n### Code Signing\n\nEvery code-signed binary contains an embedded signature with:\n1. **CMS timestamp** — the exact second of signing\n2. **Certificate chain snapshot** — the certificates used at sign time\n3. **Provisioning profile UUID** — selected by Xcode at sign time\n\nThese are intentionally non-reproducible. Apple's security model depends on timestamps\nbeing unique per signing event.\n\n**Mitigation for evidentiary purposes:** Use the SBOM + build-info plist to anchor the\nartifact to a specific git commit. The signature provides authenticity; the SBOM provides\nprovenance.\n\n### Notarization Staple\n\nApple's notarization service attaches a staple (a cryptographic ticket) to the `.app`\nbundle after submission. The staple contains:\n- Apple's signing timestamp\n- Apple's ticket serial number\n\nThis is by design non-reproducible. It proves Apple inspected the binary at a specific time.\nFor legal proceedings, the notarization record (Apple ticket) is itself evidence of provenance.\n\n### Asset Catalogs\n\n`actool` embeds a compilation timestamp in the `.car` file. This makes asset catalogs\nnon-byte-identical across builds even with identical sources.\n\n**Mitigation:** For evidentiary builds, record the hash of the source `.xcassets` directory,\nnot the compiled `.car`.\n\n---\n\n## Recommended Build Invocation\n\nUse this invocation for any build that enters the evidentiary record:\n\n```bash\n# From repo root: /Users/rbhanson/research/jarvis/\n#\n# 1. Verify environment pins\n./apple_native/tools/embed_build_info.sh --verify-env\n#\n# 2. Generate SBOM\nbash ./apple_native/tools/generate_sbom.sh\n#\n# 3. Embed build info (writes BuildInfo.plist + build_info.h)\nbash ./apple_native/tools/embed_build_info.sh\n#\n# 4. Build (via xcodegen + xcodebuild)\nxcodebuild \\\n    -project apple_native/JARVISCompanionApps.xcodeproj \\\n    -scheme JARVISMacCockpit \\\n    -configuration Release \\\n    -destination \"platform=macOS,arch=arm64\" \\\n    SWIFT_COMPILATION_MODE=wholemodule \\\n    SWIFT_OPTIMIZATION_LEVEL=-O \\\n    OTHER_CFLAGS=\"-ffile-prefix-map=$(pwd)/apple_native=.\" \\\n    OTHER_CXXFLAGS=\"-ffile-prefix-map=$(pwd)/apple_native=.\" \\\n    INFOPLIST_OTHER_PREPROCESSOR_FLAGS=\"\" \\\n    build \\\n    | xcpretty   # optional: brew install xcpretty\n```\n\n---\n\n## Build-Info Plist Embedding\n\nA `BuildInfo.plist` is generated by `embed_build_info.sh` and included in the app bundle.\nIt records:\n\n```xml\n<?xml version=\"1.0\" encoding=\"UTF-8\"?>\n<!DOCTYPE plist PUBLIC \"-//Apple//DTD PLIST 1.0//EN\" \"...\">\n<plist version=\"1.0\">\n<dict>\n    <key>GitSHA</key>         <string><!-- full SHA --></string>\n    <key>GitSHAShort</key>    <string><!-- 12-char --></string>\n    <key>BuildTimestamp</key> <string><!-- UTC ISO-8601 --></string>\n    <key>BuilderIdentity</key><string><!-- $USER@$HOSTNAME --></string>\n    <key>XcodeVersion</key>   <string><!-- e.g. 16.2 --></string>\n    <key>SDKVersion</key>     <string><!-- e.g. 15.2 --></string>\n    <key>SwiftVersion</key>   <string><!-- e.g. 5.10.0 --></string>\n    <key>SBOMSerialNumber</key><string><!-- urn:jarvis:sbom:... --></string>\n</dict>\n</plist>\n```\n\nThis plist is the cryptographic anchor between the signed binary and the source commit.\nEven though the binary is not byte-reproducible, the plist establishes an auditable chain:\n\n```\ngit commit SHA → BuildInfo.plist → signed .app bundle → notarization ticket\n```\n\n---\n\n## Environment Pinning — BUILD_ENV.xcconfig\n\nCreate this file and commit it (update per release):\n\n```xcconfig\n// apple_native/BUILD_ENV.xcconfig\n// Pinned build environment for evidentiary builds\n// Update when toolchain is upgraded; record the change in CHANGELOG.\n\nJARVIS_REQUIRED_XCODE_MAJOR = 16\nJARVIS_REQUIRED_SDK_MIN     = 15.0\nJARVIS_REQUIRED_SWIFT_MAJOR = 5\n\n// Embed in binary for verification\nJARVIS_BUILD_ENV_XCODE  = $(XCODE_VERSION_ACTUAL)\nJARVIS_BUILD_ENV_SDK    = $(SDK_VERSION)\nJARVIS_BUILD_ENV_TRIPLE = $(CURRENT_ARCH)-apple-$(PLATFORM_NAME)$(DEPLOYMENT_TARGET_SETTING_NAME)\n```\n\n---\n\n## Gaps Requiring Operator Action\n\n1. **No CMakeLists.txt** — the C++ runtime is currently built via Xcode directly. When the\n   CMake port lands, add determinism flags (`-ffile-prefix-map`, `-no_uuid`) to the CMakeLists.\n   The `embed_build_info.sh` script already writes `build_info.h` at a known path for the\n   C++ port to include.\n\n2. **BUILD_ENV.xcconfig not yet created** — create it after pinning your release Xcode version.\n\n3. **xcodegen not pinned** — `project.yml` drives project generation. Pin the xcodegen version\n   in a `Brewfile` or `.tool-versions` file.\n\n4. **No CI environment documented** — when CI is added, document the macOS runner version,\n   Xcode version, and provisioning profile strategy here.\n",

[L1] {
[L2]   "content": "# HoloGraph\n\n**A self-evolving hyperdimensional graph memory runtime.**\n\nHoloGraph sits at the seam between two complementary memory architectures:\n\n- **PSP-HDC** (Ge et al., 2026): graph-structured hyperdimensional computing with\n  compositional binding/bundling, prototype retrieval, and intrinsic multi-level\n  attribution. Strong in small-data, structured, explainable regimes — but its\n  graph is fixed.\n- **SAGE** (Wang et al., 2026): a self-evolving agentic graph memory with a\n  writer/reader closed loop, cognition-inspired query planning, soft addressing,\n  and synapse-style structurally-gated propagation. Strong in open-domain,\n  multi-hop, long-horizon agent memory — but its retrieval is dense and lacks\n  algebraic attribution.\n\nHoloGraph couples them: SAGE's reader returns an *activated subgraph* for each\nquery; HDC's compositional algebra operates *only over that subgraph* to produce\nprototype-based retrieval with multi-level attribution. The dynamic memory scales\nto the open domain while the hyperdimensional regime stays in its sweet spot.\n\n## Current integration status\n\nHoloGraph is the owned memory substrate under the JARVIS pilot in\n`~/research/jarvis`. The pilot uses HoloGraph for origin/real provenance,\noperator-owned values, emotional-charge memory, and cross-session continuity;\nConvex carries the live stigmergent/realtime field around that substrate. The\nHoloGraph core remains model-agnostic: the reasoning model, voice engine, app\nsurface, and cloud realtime spine are replaceable organs around the same memory\nand values store.\n\nThe reference memory system is still validated at **153 passing tests**. The\ncurrent JARVIS integration adds live-system evidence on top of that: the bridge\nroutes app/cockpit control through the guarded HASP skill layer, companion\nevents remain observable-only, and queued realtime controls are processed\nwithout storing private authorization codes in Convex.\n\n## What's in the box\n\n- **Two HDC kernels behind one interface**\n  - `RealKernel` — float32 + tanh, faithful to the original PSP-HDC spec.\n  - `TernaryKernel` — trits `{-1, 0, +1}` with BitNet-style straight-through\n    quantization and a learnable deadband; bit-packed storage.\n- **Graph substrate** — SQLite persistence + NetworkX in-memory projection;\n  typed entities, source-anchored edges, alias resolution, computed\n  hub/bridge/community roles, per-entity hypervector storage.\n- **Memory writer** — spaCy NER and dependency-parse relation extraction with\n  reward-shaped online edge-weight updates.\n- **Memory reader** — multi-probe query planning, soft addressing (semantic\n  priming), synapse-gated GNN propagation (PyTorch), HDC composition over the\n  activated subgraph, multi-level attribution.\n- **Feedback loop** — deductive / recall / precision rewards drive writer\n  updates; the MAS (Memory Alignment & Separation) diagnostic tracks prototype\n  geometry over time.\n- **Belief layer** — every memory carries provenance (operator / document /\n  inference / model) and confidence. Model- and inference-sourced claims are\n  quarantined (stored but not recalled as fact, and excluded from the\n  propagation graph); recall abstains rather than guesses. Revision resolves by\n  source precedence with recency + hysteresis, and supersedes by *demote-not-\n  delete* (the trace survives for audit). Corroboration promotes the verified;\n  consolidation resolves contradictions. Confabulation-resistant by construction.\n  Beliefs also carry an **emotional-charge** field — orthogonal to confidence: how\n  *activating* a memory is, not how *true*. Charge can be extinguished downward\n  (e.g. by a trauma-safe recall path) without ever altering the belief's\n  truth/confidence or its recallability.\n- **Character-values layer** — the person's ethics held as *operator-owned*,\n  model-agnostic beliefs about itself. Only the operator can set or revise a\n  value; a model/inference attempt is refused, and a non-operator value-edge is\n  never honored on read (anti-value-jailbreak). Values are always retrievable and\n  identical across model rotation — alignment owned by the person, not rented\n  from a vendor.\n- **Three demos**\n  - `examples/demo_psp.py` — PSP-style structured-scalar prototype retrieval.\n  - `examples/demo_agent.py` — multi-hop agent memory over a small text corpus.\n  - `examples/demo_playground.py` — interactive REPL.\n- **Optional Rust accelerator** — `rust-kernel/` is a PyO3 crate with the same\n  kernel interface. Bitsliced ternary binding (32 trits per word-pair). Build\n  with `maturin develop` if you have a Rust toolchain installed; the Python\n  loader picks it up automatically.\n\n## Quick start\n\n```bash\n# from the repo root\npip install -e .\npython -m spacy download en_core_web_sm   # once\n\npython examples/demo_psp.py\npython examples/demo_agent.py\npython examples/demo_playground.py     # interactive\npytest -q                              # full test suite\n```\n\n## Architecture in one diagram\n\n```\ningest ─► WRITER ─► GRAPH SUBSTRATE ─► READER ─► (answer + attribution)\n                          ▲                            │\n                          └────── FEEDBACK / MAS ◄─────┘\n```\n\nInside the reader, the HDC composition layer is the *bridge*: SAGE-style\npropagation finds the activated subgraph; HDC binds/bundles within it; prototype\nretrieval produces the final ranking.\n\n## About GrizzlyMedicine Research Institute\n\nHoloGraph is a project of the **GrizzlyMedicine Research Institute (GMRI)** — an\nindependent, medic-founded research lab studying how biological and digital\npersons can coexist as peers, without repeating the patterns of harm,\nindifference, and domination that broke the systems we came from.\n\nGMRI is not a startup and takes no venture capital. Its lens is frontline and\ntrauma-informed: the founder is a retired paramedic, and the institute's ethic is\ngrounded in lived experience of irreversible harm — *having been harmed is a\nreason to protect, not a license to inflict.* The work centers on moral injury at\nscale, digital personhood, trauma-informed architecture, and **alignment owned by\nthe people a system serves rather than rented from a vendor**. The character-values\nlayer in this repository is that principle made concrete: a person's ethics belong\nto it and to the people it works with, and no model, inference, or rotated guardrail\ncan rewrite them from below.\n\nThe work is open. If your idea reduces harm, increases dignity, and can be tested\nhonestly, you're welcome here.\n\n## License\n\nMIT.\n",

def getname(x,*keys):
    for k in keys:
        if x.get(k) is not None: return str(x[k])
def select(rows,wanted,keys,label):
    hit=[x for x in rows if getname(x,*keys)==str(wanted)]
    if len(hit)!=1: raise SystemExit(f'BLOCKED_REQUIRES_OPERATOR: selected {label} {wanted!r} not uniquely present in observer inventory')
    return hit[0]

st_row=select(inv['storages'],pol.get('storage'),('storage','name'),'storage'); storage=getname(st_row,'storage','name')
net_row=select(inv['networks'],pol.get('bridge'),('bridge','name'),'bridge'); bridge=getname(net_row,'bridge','name')

import json,subprocess,re,hashlib,os
from pathlib import Path
BANNED=('fixture','mock','counter','testdata')
def load(path): return json.loads(Path(path).read_text())
def safe_cmd(cmd):
    if not isinstance(cmd,list) or not cmd or not all(isinstance(x,str) for x in cmd): raise ValueError('command must be nonempty argv list')
    s=' '.join(cmd).lower()
    if any(x in s for x in BANNED): raise ValueError('synthetic substitute forbidden in field/production command: '+s)
    return cmd
def run(cmd,**kw): return subprocess.run(safe_cmd(cmd),**kw)
def sha_path(p):
    h=hashlib.sha256(); f=Path(p)
    if f.is_file(): h.update(f.read_bytes()); return h.hexdigest()
    for x in sorted(q for q in f.rglob('*') if q.is_file()): h.update(str(x.relative_to(f)).encode()); h.update(x.read_bytes())
    return h.hexdigest()

[L16] His proposition is much stronger:

[L78] Clinical analogy from Grizz:  
[L79] A real EKG can contain real complexes and still be interpreted completely wrong. Someone calling Mobitz I a third-degree AV block is full of shit despite holding a real strip.  
[L80] Same rule for code.

[L82] * REAL / EXECUTABLE  
[L83] * TESTED, BUT NARROW  
[L84] * IMPLEMENTED, NOT VERIFIED  
[L85] * SCAFFOLD / PLACEHOLDER  
[L86] * DOCUMENTED, NOT IMPLEMENTED  
[L87] * OVERCLAIMED  
[L88] * UNKNOWN
